\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\left|\langle\Delta_{\Theta}^{(0,\tau)},E^{(0)}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\|E^{(0)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\|\Delta_{\Theta}^{(0,\tau)}\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}

where inequality (i) comes from the fact that nuclear norm is the duel norm of operator norm. We can find a constant
C_{3}
that does not depend on
N,T,\tau_{NT}
, such that
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\|E^{(0)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{3}\sqrt{N+\mu_{NT}}
, based on
8
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\|\Delta_{\Theta}^{(0,\tau)}\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{nuc}}

(B.25)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}-M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{nuc}}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\left((1+c_{0})\|\Delta_{\Theta}-M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}+c_{0}\sqrt{NT}\|\Delta_{\beta}\|\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\left((1+c_{0})\sqrt{2R}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}-M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{F}}+c_{0}\sqrt{NT}\|\Delta_{\beta}\|\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(v)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)}\left((1+c_{0})\sqrt{2R}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{F}}+c_{0}\sqrt{NT}\|\Delta_{\beta}\|\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(vi)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{(1+c_{0})\sqrt{2R}}_{C_{4}}\sqrt{NT\omega}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{4}\sqrt{NT\omega}

Inequality (i) follows from Lemma
E.5
since
\Delta_{\Theta}^{(0,\tau)}
can be regarded as a submatrix of
\Delta_{\Theta}
. Inequality (ii) is an application of triangle inequality, and inequality (iii) comes from
(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{C}
. Inequality (iv) holds because
\Delta_{\Theta}-M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}
is a matrix of rank at most
2R
. Inequality (v) follows from the fact
\|\Delta_{\Theta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\|\Delta_{\Theta}-M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|M_{\Lambda_{0}}\Delta_{\Theta}M_{\Gamma_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
. Finally, the last inequality directly comes from
(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{N}(\omega)
.

The same argument can be applied on
s=1
. Combining equations (
B.19
)—(
B.25
), we derive the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}\widetilde{Z}^{(s)}(\omega)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}\tau_{NT}}{2}\left(C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{NT\omega}{\tau_{NT}}}+C_{3}C_{4}\sqrt{NT(N+\mu_{NT})\omega}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{2}\sqrt{NT\tau_{NT}\omega}+\frac{C_{1}C_{3}C_{4}}{2}\sqrt{NT(N+\mu_{NT})\tau_{NT}^{2}\omega},\quad s\in\{0,1\}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}Z(\omega)\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}C_{2}\sqrt{NT\tau_{NT}\omega}+C_{1}C_{3}C_{4}\sqrt{NT(N+\mu_{NT})\tau_{NT}^{2}\omega}+C_{0}(NT)^{2}\frac{\phi(\tau_{NT})}{\tau_{NT}}\omega+2\sigma N\tau_{NT}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(C_{1}C_{2}+C_{1}C_{3}C_{4})\sqrt{NT(N+\mu_{NT})\tau_{NT}^{2}\omega}+C_{0}(NT)^{2}\frac{\phi(\tau_{NT})}{\tau_{NT}}\omega+2\sigma N\tau_{NT}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{0}}{8}NT\omega+\left(\frac{8}{\kappa_{0}}(C_{1}C_{2}+C_{1}C_{3}C_{4})^{2}+2\sigma\right)(N+\mu_{NT})\tau_{NT}^{2}+C_{0}(NT)^{2}\frac{\phi(\tau_{NT})}{\tau_{NT}}\omega

When
\phi(\tau_{NT})=e^{-\zeta_{0}\tau_{NT}}
, let
\tau_{NT}=\frac{2}{\zeta_{0}}\log(NT)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}Z(\omega)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{0}}{8}NT\omega+\underbrace{\frac{4}{\zeta_{0}^{2}}\left(\frac{8}{\kappa_{0}}(C_{1}C_{2}+C_{1}C_{3}C_{4})^{2}+2\sigma+C_{0}\right)}_{\eta}(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa_{0}}{8}NT\omega+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

Substituting
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}Z(\omega)
in the concentration bound with the right-hand side of the inequality derived above, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}\left(Z(\omega)\geq\frac{\kappa_{0}}{8}NT\omega+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}+\delta\right)\leq\exp\left(-L^{-1}\min\left\{\frac{\delta}{\sigma},\frac{\delta^{2}}{NT\sigma^{2}}\right\}\right),\quad\forall\delta>0

Let
\delta=\frac{\kappa_{0}}{8}NT\omega
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}\left(Z(\omega)\geq\frac{\kappa_{0}}{4}NT\omega+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{\kappa_{0}NT\omega}{8L\sigma},\frac{\kappa_{0}^{2}NT\omega^{2}}{64L\sigma^{2}}\right\}\right)

(B.26)

Step 4 (Peeling)

For
\ell=1,2,\ldots,\infty
, define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\ell}=\left\{(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{C}\mid 2^{\ell-1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(NT)}{NT}}\leq\|\Delta_{\beta}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{NT}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq 2^{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{\log(NT)}{NT}}\right\}

It can be readily verified that
\mathcal{C}\subset\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{D}_{\ell}
. Let
\omega_{\ell}=2^{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{\log(NT)}{NT}}
, define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\ell}=\big\{Z(\omega_{\ell})\geq\frac{\kappa_{0}}{4}NT\omega_{\ell}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}\big\}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}=\bigg\{

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2},

\displaystyle\exists(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{D}_{\ell}\bigg\}

One can easily observe that when
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}
happens, since

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}\geq NT\kappa_{0}\left(\|\Delta_{\beta}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{NT}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)\geq 2^{\ell-1}\kappa_{0}NT\sqrt{\frac{\log(NT)}{NT}}

we must have

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}|

\displaystyle\geq 2^{\ell-2}\kappa_{0}NT\sqrt{\frac{\log NT}{NT}}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\kappa_{0}}{4}NT\omega_{\ell}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow Z(\omega_{\ell})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\kappa_{0}}{4}NT\omega_{\ell}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

Therefore,
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}\subset\mathcal{E}_{\ell}
. In addition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}\bigg(

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{V}}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}|

(B.27)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2},\exists(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{C}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}\left(\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}\right)\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\ell}\right)\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{V}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\kappa_{0}NT\omega_{\ell}}{8\Phi\sigma}\right)+\sum_{\ell}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\kappa_{0}^{2}NT\omega_{\ell}^{2}}{64\Phi\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\kappa_{0}2^{\ell}\sqrt{NT\log(NT)}}{8\Phi\sigma}\right)+\sum_{\ell}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\kappa_{0}^{2}4^{\ell}\log(NT)}{64\Phi\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0

where inequality (1) follows probability bound (
B.26
).

Step 5

Combining the lower bound (
B.18
) and (
B.27
), the following inequality holds for all
(\Delta_{\beta},\Delta_{\Theta})\in\mathcal{C}
wpa1:

\displaystyle|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}+\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

which implies wpa1:

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}-\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

and consequently:

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}+\Delta_{\theta_{it}})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{0}\left(NT\|\Delta_{\beta}\|^{2}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{\Theta}|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)-\eta(N+T)(\log(NT))^{2}

Since the inequality above holds for any
\mathcal{V}
, we can omit the subscript
\mathcal{V}
and obtain the same bound under
\mathbb{P}
.
Let
\kappa=\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{0}
, and the proof is completed.

Appendix C
Proofs of Local Convexity and Asymptotic Equivalence

We start with clarifying some related issues before proceeding with the proof.

First, we introduce the following abbreviations without causing ambiguity: for any
(\beta,\lambda_{i},\gamma_{t})
, define
\dot{\ell}_{it}=\dot{\ell}(X_{it}^{\prime}\beta+\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\gamma_{t})
and
\ddot{\ell}_{it}=\ddot{\ell}(X_{it}^{\prime}\beta+\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\gamma_{t})
. When the log-likelihood is evaluated at the (normalized) true parameters, define
\dot{\ell}_{it}^{0}=\dot{\ell}(X_{it}^{\prime}\beta_{0}+\lambda_{0,i}^{\prime}\gamma_{0,t})
and
\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}=\ddot{\ell}(X_{it}^{\prime}\beta_{0}+\lambda_{0,i}^{\prime}\gamma_{0,t})
. We define the following quantities,
\Delta_{\beta}=\beta-\beta_{0}
,
\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}=\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{G}_{0,t}
, and
\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}=\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}
, to denote the deviations of the parameters from their true values. When considering the difference between
\ddot{\ell}_{it}
and
\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}
, we use

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}=\ddot{\ell}_{it}-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}=\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}(\Delta_{\beta}^{\prime}X_{it}+\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}+\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma_{t}})

to represent the Taylor expansion. Here,
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
denotes the third-order derivative of
\ell_{it}
evaluated at
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
, lying on the segment between
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)
and the normalized true parameters

(\beta,\Lambda_{0}^{G},\Gamma_{0}^{G})
. Since the parameter space of
(\beta_{0},\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0})
is compact and all singular values of
\hat{G}
are uniformly bounded and strictly positive wpa1, the normalized true parameters

(\beta,\Lambda_{0}^{G},\Gamma_{0}^{G})
lie within a compact space wpa1. Thus,

(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
also lie within a compact space.

Second, addition effort is required to address the issue where, for some
i
,
t
, the nuisance parameter estimator may significantly differ from the true nuisance parameter. This challenge arises because the definition of neighborhood
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
(
3.5
) only ensures that, for any
(\Lambda,\Gamma)
in the neighborhood, the distance between
(\Lambda,\Gamma)
and
(\Lambda_{0}^{G},\Gamma^{G}_{0})
would be shrinking in terms of the Frobenius norm, but it does not guarantee that the possible nuisance parameter is accurate for each individual
i,t
. Therefore, for each
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
, we divide
\Lambda
into two parts, with subscripts for each part respectively given as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{NT}=\left\{1\leq i\leq N:\|\lambda_{i}-{\lambda}^{G}_{0,i}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}\delta_{NT}}\right\},\quad\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}=\{1,\ldots,N\}\backslash\mathcal{I}_{NT}

(C.1)

Similarly, we divide
\Gamma
into two parts, with subscripts for each part respectively given as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{NT}=\left\{1\leq t\leq T:\|\gamma_{t}-{\gamma}^{G}_{0,t}\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}\delta_{NT}}\right\},\quad\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}=\{1,\ldots,T\}\backslash\mathcal{T}_{NT}

(C.2)

It is straightforward to observe that, when
N\sim T
, for any
i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}
, the distance between
\Lambda_{i}
and
\lambda^{G}_{0,i}
converges to zero at the rate
T^{-1/4}\log(NT)
. Similarly, for any
t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}
, the distance between
\gamma_{t}
and
\gamma_{0,t}
also converges to zero as
N,T\rightarrow\infty
at the rate
N^{-1/4}\log(NT)
. Note that the notations of
\mathcal{I}_{NT}
and
\mathcal{T}_{NT}
are not rigorous, because for different
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
, we have different
\mathcal{I}_{NT}
and
\mathcal{T}_{NT}
. However, following directly from the Frobenius norm convergence rate we can easily show that the sizes of
\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}
and
\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}
are uniformly bounded for every possible
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}|\lesssim NT\delta_{NT}^{4},\quad|\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}|\lesssim NT\delta_{NT}^{4},\quad\text{wpa1}

(C.3)

Therefore, we will continue to use this imprecise notation in the following text for simpler notations.
In addition, our construction (
C.3
) implies that the sizes of
\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}
and
\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}
are at most of the order
\sqrt{N}(\log(NT))^{4}
when
N\sim T
. Thus, the number of “divergent” nuisance parameters grows at a much slower rate relative to
N
and
T
.

Third, for notational simplicity, we rescale the Hessian matrix
\mathcal{H}_{NT}
as
\mathcal{H}=NT\mathcal{H}_{NT}
and suppress the dependence on
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)
when it does not cause ambiguity. Since the only difference between
\mathcal{H}_{NT}
and
\mathcal{H}
is the factor
NT
, we will study the property of
\mathcal{H}
in the following context. Furthermore, we define:

\displaystyle H=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix},\quad F=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&F_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&F_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&0\end{pmatrix}\quad V=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&V_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&V_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&V_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&V_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}

Here,
H+F
is the Hessian related to the negative log-likelihood function
\mathcal{L}_{NT}
, and
V
is Hessian related to the penalty term.
H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}
is a
d_{X}\times d_{X}
matrix,
H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}
is a
d_{X}\times NR
matrix consisting of
N
blocks, each of size
d_{X}\times R
, and
H_{\beta\gamma^{\prime}}
is a
d_{X}\times TR
matrix with
T
blocks, each of size
d_{X}\times R
.
H_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}},V_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
are block-diagonal
NR\times NR
matrices consisting of
N^{2}
blocks, each of size
R\times R
,
H_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}},V_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}
are block-diagonal
TR\times TR
matrices with
T^{2}
blocks, each of size
R\times R
. In addition,
H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}},V_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}},F_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}
are
NR\times TR
matrices consisting of
NT
blocks, each with size
R\times R
.
More specifically:

\displaystyle H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}X^{\prime}_{it}

(C.4)

\displaystyle H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma^{\prime}_{t}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N}

\displaystyle H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\right]_{t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle H_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left\{\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N}\right\}

\displaystyle H_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left\{\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\right]_{t=1,2,\ldots,T}\right\}

\displaystyle H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma_{t}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle V_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{N}\left[\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right]_{i,i^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,N\ldots}

\displaystyle V_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\lambda_{i}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle V_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{T}\left[\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right]_{t,t^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle F_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\dot{\ell}_{it}\mathbb{I}_{R}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

We use
\hat{V}
to denote the matrix
V
with all its elements replaced by
\hat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc},i}
and
\hat{\gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc},t}
. Specifically:

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{N}\left[\hat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc},i}\hat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc},i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right]_{i,i^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,N\ldots}

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\hat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc},i}\hat{\gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc},t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{T}\left[\hat{\gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc},t}\hat{\gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc},t^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right]_{t,t^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,T}

In addition, we use
H_{0},F_{0},V_{0}
when the matrices
H,F,V
are evaluated at the normalized true value
(\beta_{0},\Lambda_{0}^{G},\Gamma_{0}^{G})
, for example,
H_{0}=H(\beta_{0},\Lambda^{G}_{0},\Gamma^{G}_{0})
. We also use
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\cdot)
to denote the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{X,\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0}}(\cdot)
when
X
is strictly exogenous, or the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{Z,\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0}}(\cdot)
when we consider predetermined covariates. It is easy to verify that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}F_{0}=0

(C.5)

By standard calculus,
\mathcal{H}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d_{X}+R(N+T))\times(d_{X}+R(N+T))}
admits the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=H+F+\hat{V}

(C.6)

We further decompose
H
by
H=\widetilde{H}+\widetilde{H}^{c}
based on partitions
\mathcal{I}_{NT}
and
\mathcal{T}_{NT}
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT},t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it})X_{it}X^{\prime}_{it}

(C.7)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\left[\boldsymbol{1}(i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT})\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma^{\prime}_{t}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\left[\boldsymbol{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT})\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\lambda^{\prime}_{i}\right]_{t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left\{\left[\boldsymbol{1}(i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT})\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N}\right\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left\{\left[\boldsymbol{1}(t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT})\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\right]_{t=1,2,\ldots,T}\right\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\left[\boldsymbol{1}(i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT},t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT})\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma_{t}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

The matrix
V
can be decomposed in the same way,
V=\widetilde{V}+\widetilde{V}^{c}
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{N}{T}\boldsymbol{1}(i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{NT})\left(\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i^{\prime}}\right)\right]_{i,i^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,N}

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{T}{N}\boldsymbol{1}(t,t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{NT})\left(\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t^{\prime}}\right)\right]_{t,t^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\boldsymbol{1}(i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT},t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT})\lambda_{i}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

C.1
Proof of Theorem
4
and
A.3

As Theorem
A.3
generalizes Theorem
4
to include predetermined covariates, we provide only the proof of the former, noting that the latter follows as a special case.

Proof of Theorem
A.3
.

It is sufficient to show
\mathcal{H}
is positive definite on the
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
. Consider the following decomposition of
\mathcal{H}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\tilde{H}^{c}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&&\tilde{H}^{c}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\hat{V}}_{S_{1}}

(C.8)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\end{pmatrix}}_{S_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\tilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&H_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&\tilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}}_{S_{3}}+(\hat{V}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\hat{V})+F

Let us first look at the first term. It is easy to verify that
S_{1}
admits the follow decomposition:

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{\hat{V}}+\underbrace{\ \begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}}_{\geq 0}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&0&\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{\hat{V}}^{c}}_{\geq 0}

(C.9)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{\hat{V}}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&0&\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}

Since matrix
\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{\hat{V}}
can be regarded as the population Hessian matrix indexed by
i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}
and
t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0}+\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{\hat{V}}

(C.10)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle C\begin{pmatrix}\left(NT-|\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}|N-|\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}|T\right)\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&\left(T-|\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}|\right)\mathrm{diag}\{D_{1},\ldots,D_{N}\}&0\\
0&0&\left(N-|\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}|\right)\mathrm{diag}\{D_{N+1},\ldots,D_{N+T}\}&\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathrm{diag}\{D_{1},\ldots,D_{N}\}&0\\
0&0&N\mathrm{diag}\{D_{N+1},\ldots,D_{N+T}\}&\end{pmatrix}

where
D_{i}=1_{\{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}\}}\mathbb{I}_{R}
for any
i=1,2,\ldots,N
, and

D_{N+t}=1_{\{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}\}}\mathbb{I}_{R}
for any
t=1,2,\ldots,T
. Inequality (i) uses Assumption
A.2
, and inequality (ii) is from the asymptotic assumption of
N,T
. In addition, by Lemma
C.1
,
we conclude that, there exists a constant
B_{1}>0
such that, with probability approaching to
1
:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&0&\widetilde{H}^{c}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\geq}}B_{1}\min\{N,T\}\mathrm{diag}\left\{0_{d_{X}},\mathbb{I}_{R}-D_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{I}_{R}-D_{N},\mathbb{I}_{R}-D_{N+1},\ldots,\mathbb{I}_{R}-D_{N+T}\right\}

(C.11)

Combining equations (
C.9
), (
C.10
), and (
C.11
), we conclude that for any
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
,
S_{1}
is locally convex wpa1. In addition, let
B_{2}=\min\{\frac{1}{2}C,B_{1}\}
irrelevant with
N,T
, for any
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
,
S_{1}
admits asymptotic block structure:

\displaystyle S_{1}\geq B_{2}\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{NR}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{TR}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{wpa1}

(C.12)

Therefore, the optimization problem is locally convex if the impact (or the maximum singular values) of the derivations,
S_{2}
,
S_{3}
,
\hat{V}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\hat{V}
, and
F
are asymptotically negligible compared to
S_{1}
. Using Lemma
C.3
we can prove that
\frac{1}{2}S_{1}+S_{2}
is positive definite. In addition, using Lemma
C.4
, and Lemma
C.5
, we have:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|S_{3}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\text{ }\|F\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

(C.13)

Using Lemma
C.6
, since
(\hat{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc}},\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
, we have wpa1

\displaystyle\|\hat{V}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\hat{V}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

(C.14)

We defer the and lemmas and their proofs to the end of this subsection. Combining (
C.12
)—(
C.14
), and Weyl’s theorem, we conclude that for any
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\geq\frac{1}{3}B_{2}\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{NR}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{TR}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{wpa1}

(C.15)

Thus,
\mathcal{H}
is positive definite and strictly convex wpa1 on neighborhood
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
. Letting
c_{5}=\frac{1}{3}B_{2}
completes the proof.

Lemma C.1

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
A.3
, there exist a constant
B_{1}>0
independent of
N,T
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&H_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&0&H_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\geq B_{1}\begin{pmatrix}0_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{NR}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{TR}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{wpa1}

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

Recall

\displaystyle H_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left\{\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N}\right\}

\displaystyle H_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{diag}\left\{\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\right]_{t=1,2,\ldots,T}\right\}

For any
i
-th diagonal block of
H_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
, with probability approaching to
1
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sigma_{\min}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\gtrsim}}\inf_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sigma_{\min}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\inf_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sigma_{\min}\left(\Gamma_{0}^{G\prime}\Gamma_{0}^{G}\right)-\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\Gamma^{\prime}\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G\prime}\Gamma_{0}^{G}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\gtrsim}}T-\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\Gamma^{\prime}\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G\prime}\Gamma_{0}^{G}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\gtrsim}}T-\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}-\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\gtrsim}}T-\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\gtrsim}}T-T\delta_{NT}

\displaystyle\gtrsim T

where inequality (i) uses Assumption
A.1
(iii)
, inequality (ii) follows from Assumption
A.1
(iv)
, inequality (iii) is Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, inequality (iv) is based on the uniform boundedness of
\Gamma,\Gamma_{0}^{G}
, and inequality (v) follows from Theorem
A.1
. We also obtain:

\displaystyle\inf_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sigma_{\min}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\right)\gtrsim N,\quad\text{wpa1}

based on the same argument. Therefore, we are able to find such constant
B_{1}>0
independent of
N,T
and complete the proof.

Lemma C.2

Under the assumptions stated in Theorem
A.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(NT\delta_{NT})

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

Observe that

\displaystyle H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime})

(C.16)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime})-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}))

We have

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(C.17)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underbrace{\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\left(\Delta_{\beta}^{\prime}X_{it}+\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{t}\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\right)}_{\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}(\Delta_{\beta}^{\prime}X_{it})X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{t}\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\right)X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle NT\delta_{NT}+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}_{t}\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\right)X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\gamma}_{t}^{\prime}\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}+NT\delta_{NT}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(v)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sqrt{N}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda^{G}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma^{G}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+NT\delta_{NT}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(vi)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle NT\delta_{NT}

Equation (i) represents the Taylor expansion, where
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
denotes the third-order derivative of
\ell_{it}
evaluated at
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
, a point on the segment between
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)
and the true parameters. As we have discussed before,
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
also lies in a compact space, as both
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
and true parameters lie within in compact spaces. Equation (ii) follows from the application of the triangular inequality. Equation (iii) is derived based on the uniform boundedness of
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
,
X
, and
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
(Assumption
A.1
(ii)
and
(iii)
), and the bound
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\beta-\beta_{0}\|\leq\delta_{NT}
. Inequality (iv) is the triangular inequality. Inequality (v) is based on the uniform boundedness of
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
,
X
, and
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
, along with the application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Inequality (vi) employs the bounds
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda^{G}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{N}\delta_{NT}
and
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma^{G}_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{T}\delta_{NT}
. A byproduct that will be frequently used in the subsequent analysis is

\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}^{2}\leq NT\delta_{NT}^{2}

which can be proved using the similar method.

Under Assumption
A.1
(i)
and
(ii)
, we employ Theorem 1 from
22
to establish that for any
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}))\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{NT})

(C.18)

By combining equations (
C.16
), (
C.17
), (
C.18
), and
\delta_{NT}\lesssim\log(NT)/\sqrt{\min\{N,T\}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O_{p}(NT\delta_{NT})

Lemma C.3

Under the conditions in Theorem
4
, for any
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
,
\frac{1}{2}S_{1}+S_{2}
is positive definite wpa1.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

As we have demonstrated in the Proof of Theorem
A.3
, there is a constant
B_{2}
such that

\displaystyle S_{1}\geq B_{2}\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{NR}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{TR}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{wpa1}

it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}S_{1}+S_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{B_{2}}{2}\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{NR}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{TR}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\geq}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{B_{2}}{3}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&\frac{B_{2}}{2}T\mathbb{I}_{R}&0\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&0&\frac{B_{2}}{2}N\mathbb{I}_{R}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{wpa1}

where the inequality (i) follows from Lemma
C.2
ensuring that
\left\|H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(NT)
. The matrix
\frac{1}{2}S_{1}+S_{2}
is positive definite if its Schur complement:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\frac{B_{2}}{2}T\mathbb{I}_{R}&0\\
0&\frac{B_{2}}{2}N\mathbb{I}_{R}\end{pmatrix}-\frac{3}{NTB_{2}}\begin{pmatrix}H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\tilde{H}_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}

is positive definite. Thus, by using Weyl’s Theorem, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}/(NT)=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

We focus the Frobenius norm instead of the spectral norm because:

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\left\|\begin{pmatrix}H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\\
H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\|H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(C.19)

Observe that:

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}=((\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})+(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})+\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))(\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}+\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t})

we have

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}+(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}+\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}

\displaystyle+(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}+\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}

where
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
denotes the third-order derivative of
\ell_{it}
evaluated at
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
, lying on the segment between
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)
and the normalized true parameters. Since both
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
and true parameters lie within in compact spaces, it follows that
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
is also in a compact space. Here, we use
\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}=\Delta_{\beta}^{\prime}X_{it}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}^{\prime}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}+\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}^{\prime}\tilde{\gamma}_{t}
to simplify the expression.
Since the
X
and
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
are uniformly bounded, and
\ell_{it}
is four times differentiable,
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
is uniformly bounded by extreme value theorem. Let us focus on
\|H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
and the bound of
\|H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
is obtained using the same method.

\displaystyle\|H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\bigg\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\Delta_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}

(C.20)

\displaystyle+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6\Bigg\{\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{A_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{A_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{A_{3}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{A_{4}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})\Delta^{\prime}_{\gamma_{t}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{A_{5}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma^{\prime}_{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{A_{6}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X_{it}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}}_{A_{7}}

The maximum singular value of the first term
A_{1}
has the following bound:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}T\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}X_{it}\gamma^{\prime}_{0,t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}^{2}

(C.21)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}T\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta})^{2}+T\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\left(\Delta_{\lambda_{i}}\|^{2}+\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim NT^{2}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\beta-\beta_{0}\|^{2}+T^{2}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}+TN\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\lesssim}}NT^{2}\delta_{NT}^{2}

where inequality (i) follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, inequality (ii) uses the boundedness of
X
,
(\beta,\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0})
,
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
, and
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
, and inequality (iii) follows directly from the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
.

For the maximum singular value of the second term
A_{2}
, we proceed as follows.

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(C.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}^{c}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(C.23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}^{c}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(C.24)

Given that (1)
\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-X_{it}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))=0
, (2)
\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-X_{it}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it})\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}
is uniformly bounded, and (3)
\{\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}\}_{1\leq t\leq T}
(conditional on
(Z,\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0})
or
(X,\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0})
) satisfies the mixing condition in Assumption
A.1
(i)
, we apply Theorem 1 from
22
to conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim NT

Additionally, by the boundedness of
(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}^{c}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim N|\mathcal{T}_{NT}^{c}|^{2}\lesssim N^{3}T^{2}\delta_{NT}^{8}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{2}\lesssim N^{3}T^{2}\delta_{NT}^{8}

The maximum singular value of
A_{3}
is bounded by:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}T\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\|^{2}\|\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}

(C.25)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}T\sum_{t=T}^{T}\left\{\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}X_{it}\|^{2}\right\}\|\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}NT^{2}\delta^{2}_{NT}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\lesssim}}NT^{3}\delta^{4}_{NT}

where inequality (i) uses the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, inequality (ii) relies on the uniform boundedness of
X
,
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
, and
\widetilde{\dddot{\ell}}_{it}
, as well as the fact that
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}^{2}\leq\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}^{2}\lesssim NT\delta_{NT}^{2}
(this be derived using the same method as in the Proof of Lemma
C.2
). Inequality (iii) follows directly from the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
.
Using similar arguments, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{4},A_{5}\lesssim NT^{2}\delta^{2}_{NT}

(C.26)

We establish the upper bounds of
A_{6}
and
A_{7}
by the boundedness condition:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim N|\mathcal{T}^{c}_{NT}|^{2}\lesssim N^{3}T^{2}\delta_{NT}^{8}

(C.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}A_{7}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{2}|\mathcal{I}^{c}_{NT}|\lesssim NT^{3}\delta_{NT}^{4}

Thus, combining (
C.20
), (
C.21
), (
C.22
), (
C.25
), (
C.26
), and (
C.27
), we conclude that wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{NT^{2}\delta_{NT}^{2},NT^{3}\delta_{NT}^{4},N^{2}T\delta_{NT}^{4},N^{3}T^{2}\delta_{NT}^{8}\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{N^{4},T^{4}\}\delta_{NT}^{4}

Based on similar arguments we also show that wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{N^{4},T^{4}\}\delta_{NT}^{4}

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}H_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}/(NT)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\min\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}\left(\log(NT)\right)^{4}\right)

(C.28)

\displaystyle=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

(C.29)

We complete the proof.

Lemma C.4

Under the conditions in Theorem
A.3
, the maximum singular value of
S_{3}
in (
C.8
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|S_{3}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

Recall

\displaystyle S_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}

The operator norm of
S_{3}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\|S_{3}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\underbrace{\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{A_{1}}+\underbrace{\left\|\begin{pmatrix}0&H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&0\end{pmatrix}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{A_{2}}

Step 1

In this step, we aim to find the upper bound of
A_{1}
. We focus on the maximum singular value of
\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
, and the singular values of
\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}
can be analyzed in the same way. Since
A_{1}
has block diagonal structure, and for any
i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}^{c}
, the
i
-diagonal
R\times R
block in
\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
is a zero matrix, it suffices to consider the
i
-diagonal
R\times R
block where
i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}
. For any
i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}
, we have

\displaystyle[\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}]_{i}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\left((-\ddot{\ell}_{it})\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}^{\prime}-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}-\mathbb{E}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}))\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}

thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|[\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}]_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{Q1}+\underbrace{\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{Q2}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{Q3}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}-\mathbb{E}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}))\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}}_{Q4}

The upper bound of
Q_{1}
is obtained as follows:

\displaystyle Q_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(C.30)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}_{NT}|}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\left\|\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\ddot{\ell}_{it}\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\lesssim}}T\delta_{NT}

where inequality (i) applies the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, inequality (ii) uses the assumption that
{\ell}_{it},\gamma_{0,t}
are uniformly bounded, and inequality (iii) follows directly from the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
. The upper bound of
Q_{2}
is obtained via the similar method:

\displaystyle Q_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(C.31)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{T}_{NT}|}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\left\|\ddot{\ell}_{it}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{T}\left\|\ddot{\ell}_{it}\Delta_{\gamma_{t}}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\delta_{NT}

where inequality (i) uses the assumption that
{\ell}_{it},\gamma_{t}
are uniformly bounded. We drive the bound of
Q_{3}
by:

\displaystyle Q_{3}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left\|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\gamma_{0,t}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

(C.32)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left|\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\tilde{\Delta}_{Y^{*}_{it}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}|X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}|+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\|\lambda_{i}\|\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\|+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\|\gamma_{0,t}\|\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}|X_{it}^{\prime}\Delta_{\beta}|+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\lambda_{i}\|\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\|+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\gamma_{0,t}\|\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle T\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Delta_{\beta}\|_{2}+\sqrt{T}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+T\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle T\delta_{NT}+T^{3/2}\delta_{NT}^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\delta_{NT}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

Therefore, combining (
C.30
), (
C.31
), and (
C.32
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|[\widetilde{H}_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}]_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

(C.33)

By the same method we also prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|[\widetilde{H}_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}]_{i}\|=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

(C.34)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

(C.35)

Step 2

In this step, we aim to bound the influence of
A_{2}
. Let us focus on
H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}
and consider the expansion of its
(i,t)
-th block,
\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\lambda_{0,i}^{G}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}
, when
i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}
and
t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}
:

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t}-\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\lambda_{0,i}^{G}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\ddot{\ell}_{it}(\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t}-\lambda_{0,i}^{G}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t})}_{H_{1,it}}+\underbrace{(\ddot{\ell}_{it}-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\lambda_{0,i}^{G}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}}_{H_{2,it}}+\underbrace{(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{\phi_{0}}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}))\lambda_{0,i}^{G}\gamma^{G\prime}_{0,t}}_{H_{3,it}}

and for notational simplicity we collect the terms above into matrices
H_{1},H_{2},H_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{RN\times RT}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|H_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\widetilde{H}_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}

(C.36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}(\|H_{1}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}+\|H_{2}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}+\|H_{3}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}})+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t})^{2}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t})^{2}\right)

We have wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|H_{1}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\|\lambda_{i}\|^{2}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\|^{2}

(C.37)

\displaystyle+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\|\gamma^{G}_{0,i}\|^{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\lambda_{i}\|^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\gamma_{0,t}^{G}\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle N\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+T\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle NT\delta_{NT}^{2}

and wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|H_{2}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}\Delta^{2}_{Y^{*}_{it}}

(C.38)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta^{2}_{Y^{*}_{it}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle NT\delta_{NT}^{2}

In addition, based on the Lemma
E.2
, we show that with probability approaching to
1
:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|H_{3}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\max\{N,T\}\log((NT))^{2}\lesssim NT\delta_{NT}^{2}

(C.39)

Finally,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{NT}}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t})^{2}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\ddot{\ell}_{it}\lambda_{i}\gamma^{\prime}_{t})^{2}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle N|\mathcal{T}_{NT}|+T|\mathcal{I}_{NT}|

(C.40)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\max\{N^{3},T^{3}\}\delta_{NT}^{4}=o_{p}(\min\{N^{2},T^{2}\})

Therefore, combining equations (
C.37
)— (
C.40
) we conclude that
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|S_{3}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

Lemma C.5

Under the conditions in Theorem
A.3
, the maximum singular value of
F
in (
C.8
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

Consider the expansion of
(i,t)
of
\dot{\ell}_{it}
(since
\mathbb{E}_{0}\dot{\ell}_{it}^{0}=0
):

\displaystyle\dot{\ell}_{it}=(\dot{\ell}_{it}-\dot{\ell}^{0}_{it})+(\dot{\ell}^{0}_{it}-\mathbb{E}\dot{\ell}_{it}^{0})

and the corresponding matrix decomposition

\displaystyle F=\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}0&F_{1,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
F_{1,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&0\end{pmatrix}}_{F_{1}}+\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}0&F_{2,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
F_{2,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&0\end{pmatrix}}_{F_{2}}

where

\displaystyle F_{1,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[(\dot{\ell}_{it}-\dot{\ell}^{0}_{it})\mathbb{I}_{R}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle F_{2,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[(\dot{\ell}^{0}_{it}-\mathbb{E}_{0}\dot{\ell}_{it}^{0})\mathbb{I}_{R}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

The operator norm of
F
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

We conclude that
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F_{1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})
using the same argument in Lemma
C.3
and
\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F_{2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\{N,T\})
based on the Lemma
E.2
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|F\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})

Lemma C.6

Under the conditions in Theorem
A.3
, we have:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\max\{N,T\})

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.

Recall that

\displaystyle V_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{T}{N}\left[\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right]_{i,i^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,N\ldots}

\displaystyle V_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\lambda_{i}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}\right]_{i=1,2,\ldots,N,t=1,2,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle V_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{N}{T}\left[\gamma_{t}\gamma_{t^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right]_{t,t^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots,T}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{\mathrm{F}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-V_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}-V_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle+2\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}-V_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

Then wpa1 we have:

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}-V_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{T^{2}}{N^{2}}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{N}\left\|\lambda_{i}\lambda_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}-\lambda_{i,0}^{G}\lambda_{i^{\prime},0}^{G\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\frac{T^{2}}{N^{2}}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{N}\left(\|\lambda_{i^{\prime}}\|^{2}\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|^{2}+\|\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|^{2}\|\lambda_{i^{\prime}}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i^{\prime}}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\frac{T^{2}}{N}\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle T^{2}\delta_{NT}^{2}

where inequality (i) employs Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and inequality (ii) uses the uniform boundedness of
\Lambda
and
\Lambda_{0}^{G}
. We prove that wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}-V_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim N^{2}\delta_{NT}^{2}

In addition, we have wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}-V_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\|\lambda_{i}\|^{2}\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\|^{2}+\|\gamma^{G}_{0,t}\|^{2}\|\lambda_{i}-\lambda^{G}_{0,i}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle N\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Gamma-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+T\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|\Lambda-\Lambda_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle NT\delta^{2}_{NT}

where inequality (i) employs Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and inequality (ii) uses the uniform boundedness of
\Lambda
and
\Gamma_{0}^{G}
. Finally, we have wpa1

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}}\|V-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\max\{N,T\}\delta_{NT}=o_{p}(\max\{N,T\})

and complete the proof.

C.2
Proof of Lemma
A.5

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{U}}(\cdot):=\mathbb{P}\left(\cdot\mid\mathcal{U}\right)
denote the probability conditional on
\mathcal{U}
defined in Assumption
A.4
, and let
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\cdot):=\mathbb{E}\left(\cdot\mid\mathcal{U}\right)
denote the expectation conditional on
\mathcal{U}
. By (
C.5
), (
C.6
), we have:

\displaystyle NT\mathbb{E}_{0}\mathcal{H}(\beta_{0},\Lambda_{0}^{G},\Gamma_{0}^{G})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}+(\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0})+V_{0}+(\mathbb{E}_{0}\hat{V}-V_{0})

In addition, by Lemma
C.6
, we have
\|\mathbb{E}_{0}\hat{V}-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\sup_{\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma\in\delta_{NT}}\|V-V_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(\max\{N,T\})
. Thus,
it suffices to show that the minimum eigenvalue of
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}H_{0}+V_{0}
is strictly positive with order
\min\{N,T\}
, and the maximum singular of the perturbation
(\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0})
is small enough compared to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}H_{0}+V_{0}
.

Step 1

In this step, we want to show that minimum eigenvalue of
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}+V_{0}
is strictly positive with order
\min\{N,T\}
. For any
i,t
,
\nabla^{2}\ell_{it}^{0}
is the
(d_{X}+R(N+T))
-dimensional squared matrix where the second derivatives are taken with respect to
(\beta,\Lambda,\Gamma)
.
\nabla^{2}(-\ell_{it}^{0})
can be regarded as the sample Hessian evaluated at the true parameters if we only have one observation
(Y_{it},X_{it})
. For notational simplicity, let
L^{0}_{it}:=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ell_{it}^{0})
. It is obvious that both
\nabla^{2}(-\ell_{it}^{0})
and
\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})
are positive semi-definite. Now let us consider the shrinking version of the
\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})
:

\displaystyle\widecheck{\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})}=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})&\nabla_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})&\nabla_{\beta\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})\\
\nabla_{\lambda\beta^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})&\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})&\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})\\
\nabla_{\gamma\beta^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})&\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})&\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})\end{pmatrix}

where
\nu
is defined in Assumption
A.4
(ii)
. Since
\sqrt{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}))
is positive semi-definite, then
\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})
is positive semi-definite as well. Thus, by Schur’s lemma,
\widecheck{\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})}
is positive semi-definte if its Schur complement
\widecheck{\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})}\backslash\sqrt{\nu}\nabla_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})
is positive semi-definite. The latter statement is true because:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}))\gamma_{t,0}\gamma_{t,0}^{\prime}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}))\gamma_{t,0}\lambda_{i,0}^{\prime}\\
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}))\lambda_{i,0}\gamma_{t,0}^{\prime}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}))\lambda_{i,0}\lambda_{i,0}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}^{\prime}))\left(\sqrt{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}))\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}))\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{0,t}\gamma_{0,t}^{\prime}&\gamma_{0,t}\lambda_{0,i}^{\prime}\\
\lambda_{0,i}\gamma_{0,t}^{\prime}&\lambda_{0,i}\lambda_{0,i}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}))-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}^{\prime}))\left(\sqrt{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}X_{it}^{\prime}))\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{E}_{0}(-\ddot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it}))\right)\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{0,t}\gamma_{0,t}^{\prime}&\gamma_{0,t}\lambda_{0,i}^{\prime}\\
\lambda_{0,i}\gamma_{0,t}^{\prime}&\lambda_{0,i}\lambda_{0,i}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0

where the last inequality comes from Assumption
A.4
(ii)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\underbrace{\widecheck{\nabla^{2}(-L^{0}_{it})}}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle=(1-\sqrt{\nu})\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
0&H_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&H_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\\
\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=(1-\sqrt{\nu})\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}+(1-\sqrt{\nu})\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&H_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&H_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\\
\end{pmatrix}

Thus, using the similar argument in Lemma 2 from
11
, there exists constant
c>0
independent of
N,T
such that:

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\nu})\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&H_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&H_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\\
\end{pmatrix}+V_{0}

\displaystyle\geq c\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{RN}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{RT}\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\nu})\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\geq c\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}

Therefore, we must have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}+V_{0}\geq c\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{RN}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{RT}\end{pmatrix}

whose minimum eigenvalue is positive with order
\min\{N,T\}
.

Step 2

In this step want to proof that the perturbation
(\mathbb{E}H_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}H_{0})
is small enough relative to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}H_{0}+bV_{0}
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}+bV_{0}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0}+bV_{0}+(E_{0}H_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0}H_{0})

(C.41)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{RN}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{RT}\end{pmatrix}+\frac{c}{2}\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}+\frac{c}{2}\begin{pmatrix}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})\begin{pmatrix}H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
H_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
H_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\end{pmatrix}+(\mathbb{E}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&H_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&H_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&H_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}\frac{c}{2}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}+(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}&(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\beta\lambda^{\prime}}&(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\beta\gamma^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\lambda\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\\
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\gamma\beta^{\prime}}&0&0\end{pmatrix}}_{A_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&H_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}&0\\
0&0&H_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}}_{A_{2}}+\underbrace{(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&0&H_{0,\lambda\gamma^{\prime}}\\
0&H_{0,\gamma\lambda^{\prime}}&0\end{pmatrix}}_{A_{3}}

\displaystyle+c\begin{pmatrix}\frac{NT}{2}\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}&0&0\\
0&T\mathbb{I}_{RN}&0\\
0&0&N\mathbb{I}_{RT}\end{pmatrix}

Here, we first study the eigenvalue values of
A_{1}
. For the upper-left block of
A
, we establish a lower bound for its minimum eigenvalue as follows:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left(\frac{1}{2}NT\mathbb{I}_{d_{X}}+(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\gtrsim}}NT+O_{p}(\sqrt{NT})\gtrsim NT

where inequality (i) follows from the fact that
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\beta\beta^{\prime}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{NT})
by Assumption
A.4
(i)
and Theorem 1 in
22
. By the same argument, each element in
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{\beta\lambda^{\prime}}
is of order
O_{p}(\sqrt{T})
, and each element in
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{\beta\gamma^{\prime}}
is of order
O_{p}(\sqrt{N})
. By applying the block version of Gershgorin’s Circle Theorem, we conclude that every eigenvalue of
A_{1}
falls into one of two categories: either it is positive and of order
O_{p}(NT)
, or it is of order
O_{p}(\sqrt{T}),O_{p}(\sqrt{N})
(with an unspecified sign). Therefore, by the asymptotic assumption about
N,T
, the minimum eigenvalue of
A_{1}
must be of order
\min\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}
.

In addition, by Assumption
A.4
(i)
and Theorem 1 from
22
, each
R
-dimensional block of
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}
must be of order
O_{p}(\sqrt{T})
. Using the same argument we can also show that each
R
-dimensional block of
(\mathbb{E}_{0}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}_{0})H_{0,\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}
must be of order
O_{p}(\sqrt{N})
. Since
A_{2}
has diagonal block structure, the minimum eigenvalue of
A_{2}
must be of order
o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})
as well. To bound the eigenvalues of
A_{3}
, by Assumption
A.4
and Lemma
E.2
, the maximum singular value of
A_{3}
must be of order
O_{p}(\sqrt{\max\{N,T\}}\log(NT))
, and thus, the minimum eigenvalue of
A_{3}
must be of order
o_{p}(\min\{N,T\})
