. Combining the previous result Weyl’s theorem, and the asymptotic assumption about
N,T
, we conclude that the minimum eigenvalue of
\mathbb{E}_{0}\mathcal{H}_{0}
is

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\mathbb{E}_{0}\mathcal{H}_{0})\gtrsim\min\{N,T\}

Since
\mathcal{H}_{NT}=\frac{1}{NT}\mathcal{H}
, it immediately follows that
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbb{E}_{0}\mathcal{H}_{0})\gtrsim(\max\{N,T\})^{-1}
.

Appendix D
Proof in Section
4

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Recall

\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{nuc}},\hat{\Theta}_{\mathrm{nuc}})\in\argmin_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}},\Theta\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times T}}\left\{\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta,\Theta)+\frac{\varphi_{NT}}{\sqrt{NT}}\|\Theta\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}\right\}

Let
h_{NT}(\Theta)=\frac{\varphi_{NT}}{\sqrt{NT}}\|\Theta\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}
, and the proximal gradient operator can be defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{prox}_{s_{\theta}h_{NT}}(\tilde{\Theta})=\argmin_{\Theta^{*}}\left\{h_{NT}(\tilde{\Theta})+\frac{1}{2}\|\tilde{\Theta}-\Theta\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right\}

By Lemma
D.1
, we can show that for any feasible
\beta_{1},\Theta_{1},\beta_{2},\Theta_{2}
in optimization (
3.1
), there exist constant
L_{\beta},L_{\theta}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{2},\Theta_{2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})+\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})^{\prime}(\beta_{2}-\beta_{1})+\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),(\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{\beta}}{2}\|\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}\|^{2}+\frac{L_{\theta}}{2NT}\|\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

With the quadratic upper bound, we can employ the standard proof. Let
G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta,\Theta)=\frac{1}{s_{\theta}}(\Theta-\mathrm{prox}_{s_{\theta}h_{NT}}(\Theta-s_{\theta}\nabla_{\Theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta,\Theta)))

and update the parameters via

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{2}\\
\Theta_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{1}-s_{\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\\
\Theta_{1}-s_{\theta}G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\end{pmatrix}

We can obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{2},\Theta_{2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})-s_{\beta}\|\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|^{2}-s_{\theta}\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{\beta}s_{\beta}^{2}}{2}\|\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|^{2}+\frac{L_{\theta}s_{\theta}^{2}}{2NT}\|G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

When
s_{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{L_{\beta}}
and
s_{\theta}\leq\frac{NT}{L_{\theta}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{2},\Theta_{2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})-s_{\theta}\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{s_{\beta}}{2}\|\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|^{2}+\frac{s_{\theta}}{2}\|G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Also, since
\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
h_{NT}(\cdot)
are convex functions, we have

\displaystyle h_{NT}(\Theta_{2})

\displaystyle\leq h_{NT}(\Theta_{0})-(G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})-\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}))^{\prime}(\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{1}+s_{\theta}G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}))

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{0},\Theta_{0})-\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})^{\prime}(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})-\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),(\Theta_{0}-\Theta_{1})\rangle

Adding these three inequality yields:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{2},\Theta_{2})+h_{NT}(\Theta_{2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{0},\Theta_{0})+h_{NT}(\Theta_{0})

\displaystyle+\nabla_{\beta}L_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})^{\prime}(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})+\langle\nabla_{\theta}L_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),\Theta_{1}-\Theta_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{s_{\beta}}{2}\|\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|^{2}-\frac{s_{\theta}}{2}\|G_{s_{\theta}}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Therefore, denote
\mathcal{\psi}^{(k)}=\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta^{(k)},\Theta^{(k)})+h_{NT}(\Theta^{(k)})
, and
\mathcal{\psi}_{0}=\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{0},\Theta_{0})+h_{NT}(\Theta_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{\psi}^{(k+1)}-\mathcal{\psi}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2s_{\beta}}\left(\|\beta^{(k)}-\beta_{0}\|^{2}-\|\beta^{(k+1)}-\beta_{0}\|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2s_{\theta}}\left(\|\Theta^{(k)}-\Theta_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}-\|\Theta^{(k+1)}-\Theta_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{\psi}^{(k)}-\mathcal{\psi}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=0}^{k}(\mathcal{\psi}^{(i)}-\mathcal{\psi}_{0})\leq\frac{1}{2ks_{\beta}}\|\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2ks_{\theta}}\|\Theta^{(0)}-\Theta_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{F}}

Thus, we can prove that when
s_{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{L_{\beta}}
and
s_{\theta}\leq\frac{NT}{L_{\theta}}
, the optimization would converge to its global minimum.

Lemma D.1
(Quadratic upperbound)

For any feasible
\beta_{1},\Theta_{1},\beta_{2},\Theta_{2}
in optimization (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{2},\Theta_{2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})+\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})^{\prime}(\beta_{2}-\beta_{1})+\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),(\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{L_{\beta}}{2}\|\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}\|^{2}+\frac{L_{\theta}}{2NT}\|\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

with
L_{\beta}=4d_{X}b_{\max}\rho_{X}^{2}
and
L_{\theta}=4b_{\max}

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{2},\Theta_{2})=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})+\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1})^{\prime}(\beta_{2}-\beta_{1})+\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta_{1},\Theta_{1}),(\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle+(\beta_{2}^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}_{1},\Theta^{\prime}_{2}-\Theta^{\prime}_{1})\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Theta})(\beta_{2}^{\prime}-\beta^{\prime}_{1},\Theta^{\prime}_{2}-\Theta^{\prime}_{1})^{\prime}

where

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta,\Theta)=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\theta^{\prime}}\\
H_{\theta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\geq 0

and

\displaystyle H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}\ddot{\ell}_{it}X^{\prime}_{it}

\displaystyle H_{\beta\theta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT}\left[X_{it}\ddot{\ell}_{it}\right]_{i=1,\ldots,N,t=1,\ldots,T}

\displaystyle H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT}\mathrm{diag}\left\{\ddot{\ell}_{it}\right\}_{i=1,\ldots,N,t=1,\ldots,T}

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}2H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}&0\\
0&2H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\beta\theta^{\prime}}\\
H_{\theta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}&-H_{\beta\theta^{\prime}}\\
-H_{\theta\beta^{\prime}}&H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\geq 0

The last inequality holds because (1)
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta,\Theta)\geq 0
and (2) flipping the sign of off-diagonal part of the matrix does not change the eigenvalues of the matrix.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}\\
\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1}\end{pmatrix}^{\prime}\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Theta})\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}\\
\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1}\end{pmatrix}\leq 2\sigma_{\max}(H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}})\|\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}\|^{2}+2\sigma_{\max}(H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}})\|\Theta_{2}-\Theta_{1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

Since
\sigma_{\max}(H_{\beta\beta^{\prime}})\leq d_{X}b_{\max}\rho_{X}^{2}
and
\sigma_{\max}(H_{\theta\theta^{\prime}})\leq\frac{b_{\max}}{NT}
, we can complete the proof.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

The proof consists of two steps. In the first step, we show that if we start with
(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})=(\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{nuc}},\hat{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc}},,\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
, then, under properly chosen step sizes
(s_{\beta},s_{\lambda},s_{\Gamma})
, the updated estimators
(\beta^{(1)},\Lambda^{(1)},\Gamma^{(1)})
will also remain in neighborhood
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
. In the second step, we establish a local quadratic upper bound for the optimization problem (
3.4
) to show that the sequence of updated estimators will always remain within the neighborhood
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
. Moreover, after each update, the updated estimate will be closer to the FE estimator than the one in the previous step.

Step 1

First, note that when evaluated at
(\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})
, we have:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda}\|\hat{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\Lambda-\Gamma^{\prime}\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=0,\quad\nabla_{\gamma}\|\hat{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\Lambda-\Gamma^{\prime}\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=0

then

\displaystyle\beta^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\beta^{(0)}-s_{\beta}\nabla_{\beta}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(0)}-s_{\lambda}\nabla_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(0)}-s_{\gamma}\nabla_{\gamma}\mathcal{L}_{NT}(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})

We have the following inequality when
s_{\beta}\lesssim 1
:

\displaystyle\|\beta^{(1)}-\beta^{(0)}\|=

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\dot{\ell}_{it}(\beta^{(0)}X_{it}+\lambda^{(0)\prime}_{i}\gamma^{(0)}_{t})X_{it}\right\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}\right\|+\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}(\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{\prime}X_{it}X_{it}\right\|

\displaystyle+\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\lambda^{(0)\prime}_{i}(\gamma_{0,t}^{G}-\gamma^{(0)}_{t})X_{it}\right\|+\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\gamma_{t}^{(0)\prime}(\lambda_{0,i}^{G}-\lambda^{(0)}_{i})X_{it}\right\|

where inequality (i) follows from the Taylor expansion and triangular inequality. Here,
\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}
is an abbreviation for
\ddot{\ell}_{it}(\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}X_{it}+\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\tilde{\gamma}_{t})
, where
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
lies between
(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})
and
(\beta_{0},\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0})
. In addition, it is easy to verify that We can establish the upper bound the preceding four terms using the similar argument in the proof of Theorem
A.3
. Combining moment condition
\mathbb{E}_{Z,\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0}}(\dot{\ell}_{it}^{0}X_{it})=0
, sampling assumption (Assumption
A.1
(i)
), and Theorem 1 in
22
yields

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}X_{it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{\log(NT)}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)

Since
\{X_{it}\}_{1\leq i\leq N,1\leq t\leq T}
,
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\Gamma})
,
(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})
, and
(\beta_{0},\Lambda_{0},\Gamma_{0})
are uniformly bounded, we obtain wpa1:

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}(\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{\prime}X_{it}X_{it}\right\|\lesssim\|\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(\delta_{NT})

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\lambda^{(0)\prime}_{i}(\gamma_{0,t}^{G}-\gamma^{(0)}_{t})X_{it}\right\|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\Gamma^{(0)}-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(\delta_{NT})

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\beta}}{NT}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\gamma_{t}^{(0)\prime}(\lambda_{0,i}^{G}-\lambda^{(0)}_{i})X_{it}\right\|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|\Lambda^{(0)}-\Lambda_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(\delta_{NT})

By the Similar method we can prove that with
\beta_{\lambda}

where equality (i) (ii) and (iii) comes from Theorem
A.1
. Therefore, we have wpa1:

\|\beta^{(1)}-\beta^{(0)}\|=o_{p}(\delta_{NT})

(D.1)

In addition, with
s_{\lambda}\sim N
:

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{(1)}-\Lambda^{(0)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\dot{\ell}_{it}(\beta^{0}X_{it}+\lambda^{G(0)\prime}_{i}\gamma^{G(0)}_{t})\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}\gamma_{0,t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}(\gamma_{0,t}-\gamma^{(0)}_{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}(\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{\prime}X_{it}\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\lambda^{(0)\prime}_{i}(\gamma_{0,t}^{G}-\gamma^{(0)}_{t})\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\gamma_{t}^{(0)\prime}(\lambda_{0,i}^{G}-\lambda^{(0)}_{i})\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}

where inequality (i) follows from the Taylor expansion and triangular inequality. By the same argument, we establish the following inequality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}\gamma_{0,t}\right\|^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}O_{p}\left(\frac{N\log(T)^{2}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}(N\delta_{NT}^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{\ell}^{0}_{it}(\gamma_{0,t}-\gamma^{(0)}_{t})\right\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{N}{T}\|\Gamma^{(0)}-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(N\delta_{NT}^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}(\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0})^{\prime}X_{it}\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}\lesssim N\|\beta^{(0)}-\beta_{0}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(N\delta_{NT}^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\lambda^{(0)\prime}_{i}(\gamma_{0,t}^{G}-\gamma^{(0)}_{t})\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{N}{T}\|\Gamma^{(0)}-\Gamma_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(N\delta_{NT}^{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{\ddot{\ell}}_{it}\gamma_{t}^{(0)\prime}(\lambda_{0,i}^{G}-\lambda^{(0)}_{i})\gamma^{(0)}_{t}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{(0)}-\Lambda_{0}^{G}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(v)}}}{{=}}o_{p}(N\delta_{NT}^{2})

where inequality (i) comes from the sampling assumption (Assumption
A.1
(i)
), and Theorem 1 in
22
, inequalities (ii)—(v) are from Theorem
A.1
. Therefore, we obtain:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|\Lambda^{(1)}-\Lambda^{(0)}\|=o_{p}(\delta_{NT})

(D.2)

Using the same method, we can also obtain:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\|\Gamma^{(1)}-\Gamma^{(0)}\|=o_{p}(\delta_{NT})

(D.3)

Combining equations (
D.1
), (
D.2
), and (
D.3
), we can show that if we start with
(\beta^{(0)},\Lambda^{(0)},\Gamma^{(0)})=(\hat{\beta}_{\mathrm{nuc}},\hat{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{nuc}},,\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
, then, under properly chosen step sizes
(s_{\beta},s_{\lambda},s_{\Gamma})
as in Theorem
6
, the updated estimators
(\beta^{(1)},\Lambda^{(1)},\Gamma^{(1)})
will also remain in neighborhood
\mathcal{B}_{\delta_{NT}}
.

Step 2

By Theorem
A.3
, the optimization is strongly convex, after each update, the updated estimate will be closer to the FE estimator (up to orthogonal transformation) than the one in the previous step. Since we have established in the first step that
(\beta^{(1)},\Lambda^{(1)},\Gamma^{(1)})\in\mathcal{B}_{\Delta_{NT}}
(
since for each
i,t
, the gradients of
\|\hat{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\Lambda-\Gamma^{\prime}\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
with respect to
\lambda_{i},\gamma_{t}
are asymptotically negligible compared to the gradients of the log-likelihood function, we can ignore the influence of the gradients of
\|\hat{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\Lambda-\Gamma^{\prime}\hat{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{nuc}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
and directly employ Algorithm
2
), the whole sequence of estimators will never jump out of the neighborhood. Therefore, we complete the proof.

Appendix E
Technical Lemmas

Lemma E.1

Suppose
X_{it}
is
d_{X}
-dimensional independent random variable bounded by
\rho_{X}>0
, and
\epsilon_{it}
is an i.i.d. Rademacher process. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}^{\prime}\epsilon_{it}\|_{2}\leq D_{1}\sqrt{NT}

where
D_{1}=\sqrt{8\pi d_{X}^{3}\rho_{X}^{2}}

Proof.
Consider
d
-th dimension of the random vector,
X_{it,d}\epsilon_{it}
.
Since
X_{it,d}
is uniformly bounded by
\rho_{X}
,
X_{it,d}\epsilon_{it}
is i.i.d. sub-Gaussian random variable with mean zero and parameter
\rho_{X}
. We obtain the Hoeffding bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it,d}\epsilon_{it}|\geq\delta\right)\leq 2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2NT\rho_{X}^{2}}}

For the concentration inequality for the sum of random vectors, let
\bar{X}_{d}
denote
\bar{X_{d}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it,d}\epsilon_{it}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}\epsilon_{it}\|_{2}\geq\delta\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\bigcup_{d=1}^{d_{X}}\{|X_{d}-\mathbb{E}X_{d}|\geq\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{d_{X}}}\}

\displaystyle\left\{\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}\epsilon_{it}\|_{2}\geq\delta\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\bigcup_{d=1}^{d_{X}}\{|X_{d}-\mathbb{E}X_{d}|\geq\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{d_{X}}}\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\mathbb{P}\left(\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}\epsilon_{it}\|_{2}\geq\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{d=1}^{d_{X}}\{|X_{d}-\mathbb{E}X_{d}|\geq\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{d_{X}}}\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bigcup_{d=1}^{d_{X}}\mathbb{P}\left(|X_{d}-\mathbb{E}X_{d}|\geq\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{d_{X}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2d_{X}\exp\left\{-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2NTd_{X}\rho_{X}^{2}}\right\}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}\epsilon_{it}\|_{2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{it}\epsilon_{it}\|_{2}\geq\delta)\mathrm{d}\delta\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}2d_{X}e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2NTd_{X}\rho_{X}^{2}}}\mathrm{d}\delta=D_{1}\sqrt{NT}

where
D_{1}=\sqrt{8\pi d_{X}^{3}\rho_{X}^{2}}
.

Lemma E.2

Consider a random matrix
Z
with dimensions
N\times T
and uniformly bounded entries. Assume that each row of
Z
is independent.
In addition, for each row
i
,
\{Z_{it}\}_{1\leq t\leq T}
is
\alpha
-mixing with mixing coefficient
\alpha_{i}(\tau)\rightarrow 0
as
\tau\rightarrow\infty
, where

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}(\tau)=\sup_{t}\sup_{A\in\mathcal{A}^{i}_{t},B\in\mathcal{B}^{i}_{t+\tau}}|\mathbb{P}(A\cap B)-\mathbb{P}(A)\mathbb{P}(B)|

where
\mathcal{A}^{i}_{t}
is the sigma-field generated by
\{\ldots,Z_{i,t-1},Z_{i,t},\}
, and
\mathcal{B}^{i}_{t+\tau}
is the sigma-field generated by
\{Z_{i,t+\tau},Z_{i,t+\tau+1},\ldots\}
. For mixing coefficients
\alpha_{i}(\tau)
,
i=1,2,\ldots,N
, we further assume that they exhibit uniformly polynomial decay rate: there exists
\beta>2
and
C>0
such that
\sup_{1\leq i\leq N}\alpha_{i}(\tau)\leq C\tau^{-\beta}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\|Z\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\max\{N,T\}}\log(N+T),\quad\text{wpa1}

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

We will employ Bernstein’s bound of the sum of rectangular matrices (proved in
36
), as stated below:

Theorem E.3
(Rectangular matrix Bernstein bound)

Consider a finite sequence
\{Z_{k}\}
of independent, random matrices with dimensions
N\times T
, Assume that each random matrix satisfies:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}Z_{k}=0,\quad\|Z_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq D

Define
\sigma^{2}=\max\left\{\left\|\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}(Z_{k}Z_{k}^{\prime})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}},\left\|\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}(Z_{k}^{\prime}Z_{k})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\right\}
.
Then for all
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\sum_{k}Z_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\delta\right)\leq(N+T)\exp\left\{-\frac{-\delta^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}+\frac{2}{3}D\delta}\right\}

To apply Theorem
E.3
, let the matrix
Z_{i}
denote the
N\times T
matrix where the
i
-th row of
Z_{i}
is the same as the
i
-th row of
Z
, and all other rows are zero. Since the entry of
Z
is uniformly bounded, there exists a constant
a_{1}>0
irrelevant with
N,T
such that

\displaystyle\|Z_{i}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq a_{1}\sqrt{T}\leq a_{1}\sqrt{\max\{N,T\}},\quad\forall i=1,2,\ldots,N

(E.1)

In addition, it is straightforward to see that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\prime})
has diagonal structure, thus, there exists a constant
a_{2}>0
irrelevant with
d_{1},d_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}(Z_{k}Z_{k}^{\prime})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq a_{2}T

(E.2)

To bound the term
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{i}(1,0)&\gamma_{i}(1,1)&\ldots&\gamma_{i}(1,T)\\
\gamma_{i}(2,1)&\gamma_{i}(2,0)&\ldots&\gamma_{i}(1,T-1)\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\gamma_{i}(T,T-1)&\gamma_{i}(T,T-2)&\ldots&\gamma_{i}(T,0)\end{pmatrix}

where
\gamma_{i}(t,\tau)=\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i,t},Z_{i,t+\tau})
for any
1\leq t\leq T-1
. By Gershgorin circle theorem (or block Gershgorin circle theorem), we can show that
\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}
is uniformly bounded by a constant
a_{3}
because

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\left\{\gamma_{i}(t,0)+2\sum_{\tau=1}^{\min\{t,T-t\}}\left|\gamma_{i}(t,\tau)\right|+\sum_{\tau=\min\{t,T-t\}+1}^{\max\{t,T-t\}}\left|\gamma_{i}(t,\tau)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\left\{\gamma_{i}(t,0)+2\sum_{\tau=1}^{T}\left|\gamma_{i}(t,\tau)\right|\right\}

In addition, since the sequence is
\alpha
-mixing with uniformly polynomial decay rate
\sup_{1\leq i\leq N}\alpha_{i}(\tau)\leq C\tau^{-\beta}
, we have (see
16
)

\displaystyle\left|\gamma_{i}(t,\tau)\right|\leq 4\rho_{Z}^{2}\alpha_{i}(\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}=4\rho_{Z}^{2}C^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}

where
\rho_{Z}>0
is a constant irrelevant with
N,T
such that
|Z_{it}|\leq\rho_{Z}
for all
i,t,N,T
. Therefore, we can further obtain that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\gamma_{i}(t,0)+\sum_{\tau=1}^{T}8\rho_{Z}^{2}C^{\frac{1}{2}}\tau^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\leq\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\gamma_{i}(t,0)+8\rho_{Z}^{2}C^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{\tau=1}^{\infty}\tau^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}

(E.3)

It is easy to check that
\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\gamma_{i}(t,0)\leq 4\rho_{Z}^{2}
, and
\sum_{\tau=1}^{\infty}\tau^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}<\infty
as
\beta>2
. Therefore, there exists a constant
c_{3}
such that
\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c_{3}
, and in addition,
\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c_{3}
.
Thus, there exists a constant
a_{d}
such that:

\displaystyle\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sum_{i}^{N}\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq a_{3}N

Moreover, let
a_{4}=2\max\{a_{2},a_{3}\}
and combine (
E.2
) and (
E.3
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}=\max\{\|\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}Z_{i}^{\prime})\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\}\leq a_{4}\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}

(E.4)

Finally, since each row of
\{Z_{i}\}_{q\leq i\leq N}
is independent and
Z=\sum_{k=1}^{d_{1}}\bar{Z}_{k}
, we employ the rectangular matrix Bernstein bound (Theorem
E.3
), inequality (
E.3
), and inequality (
E.4
) to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|Z\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\max\{N,T\}\exp\left\{-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2a_{4}\max\{N,T\}+\frac{2}{3}a_{1}\sqrt{\max\{N,T\}}\delta}\right\}

Let
\delta=\max\{4\sqrt{a_{4}},4\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}a_{1}\}\sqrt{\max\{N,T\}}\log(N+T)
, since

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|Z\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\max\{N,T\}\exp\left\{-2\log(N+T)\right\}\leq 2\exp\left\{-\log(N+T)\right\}\rightarrow 0

we can show that
\|Z\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\max\{N,T\}}\log(N+T)
with probability approaching to
1
.

Lemma E.4

For any block matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{(n+m)\times(n+m)}

\displaystyle M=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
B^{\prime}&0\end{pmatrix}

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a positive definite matrix,
B\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
, we want to prove that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(M)\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sigma_{\min}(A)-\sqrt{\sigma^{2}_{\min}(A)+s_{\max}^{2}(B)}\right)

(E.5)

where
\sigma(\cdot)
denotes the eigenvalue and
s(\cdot)
denotes the singular value.

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.

When
B=0
, the inequality (
E.5
) is directly verified since
\sigma_{\min}(M)=0
. For
B\neq 0
,
M
must admit negative eigenvalues, and we denote it as
\varsigma<0
.
Suppose the corresponding eigenvector of
\varsigma
is
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{\prime}
, by the definition of eigenvalue, we have

\displaystyle M(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{\prime}=\varsigma(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})^{\prime}\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}Ax+By=\varsigma x\\
B^{\prime}x=\varsigma y\end{array}\right.

Replacing
y=\varsigma^{-1}B^{\prime}x
into
Ax+By=\varsigma x
yields (since
\varsigma\neq 0
):

\displaystyle\varsigma^{2}x^{\prime}x-\varsigma x^{\prime}Ax-x^{\prime}BB^{\prime}x=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad\varsigma=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{x^{\prime}Ax}{x^{\prime}x}-\sqrt{\left(\frac{x^{\prime}Ax}{x^{\prime}x}\right)^{2}+4\frac{x^{\prime}BB^{\prime}x}{x^{\prime}x}}\right)

we drop the positive solution of
\varsigma
by only considering the case
\varsigma<0
. It is straightforward to check that
\varsigma
is an increasing function of
\frac{x^{\prime}Ax}{x^{\prime}x}
and a decreasing function of
\frac{x^{\prime}BB^{\prime}x}{x^{\prime}x}
. Since

\displaystyle\frac{x^{\prime}Ax}{x^{\prime}x}\geq\min_{x}\frac{x^{\prime}Ax}{x^{\prime}x}=\sigma_{\min}(A),\quad\frac{x^{\prime}BB^{\prime}x}{x^{\prime}x}\leq\max_{x}\frac{x^{\prime}BB^{\prime}x}{x^{\prime}x}=s^{2}_{\max}(B)

we conclude that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(M)\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sigma_{\min}(A)-\sqrt{\sigma^{2}_{\min}(A)+s_{\max}^{2}(B)}\right)

Lemma E.5

For any block matrix
A=[A_{1},A_{2}]
, where
A_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m_{1}},A_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m_{2}}
, we have
s_{r}(A)\geq s_{r}(A_{1})
, for
r=1,\ldots,\min\{n,m_{1}+m_{2}\}
. Thus,
\|A\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}\geq\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.5
.

For the largest singular value, let
u=(u_{1}^{\prime},u_{2}^{\prime})^{\prime}
, where
u_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
,
u_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}

\displaystyle s_{1}(A_{1})=\max_{\|u_{1}\|_{2}=1}\|A_{1}u_{1}\|_{2}=\max_{\|u_{1}\|_{2}=1,\|u_{2}\|_{2}=0}\|Au\|_{2}\leq\max_{\|u\|_{2}=1}\|Au\|_{2}=s_{1}(A)

For the second singular value, let
\tilde{u}=(\tilde{u}_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{u}_{2})^{\prime}
be the right singular vector of
A
, and consider the variational representation of singular values:

\displaystyle s_{2}(A_{1})=\min_{\dim(V)=1}\max_{u_{1}\in V^{\perp}}\|A_{1}u_{1}\|_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\max_{\|u_{1}\|=1,\langle u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1}\rangle=0}\|A_{1}u_{1}\|_{2}\leq\max_{\|u\|=1,\langle u,\tilde{u}\rangle=0}\|Au\|_{2}=s_{2}(A)

where inequality (i) follows when
V=\text{span}\{\tilde{u}_{1}\}
. We can extend the proof to show that for any
r=1,\ldots,\min\{n,m_{1}+m_{2}\}
,
s_{r}(A)\geq s_{r}(A_{1})
, and thus,
\|A\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}\geq\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{nuc}}
.
